He denounced the railway company, and heaped imprecations on the head of the driver, and talked of claiming damages. Thus he grumbled all the way, not noticing in the least where he was going, and so inamiable did his conduct appear to me that I began to enjoy the trick I was playing on him.

===Chinese===
我发觉黑德利先生不是个好相处的人；的确，他一路上抱怨个不停。他通报了自己的姓名，我刻意应了一句：“那位有名的古物学家？”从此我便不得不听他历数与书商们的种种难解难分的纠葛——照他说，那些人待他糟糕透顶。此人一身，便够《作家的易怒》（the Irritability of Authors）专写一章。他告诉我，他好几次眼看就要造就几家书局的大财运，只因他们恩将仇报至极，才不得不弃了那番宏图。除了这些陈年冤史和方才车夫的新祸，他还有一桩苦情要诉：坐火车来时，他正削着一支铅笔，机车进站时猛地一顿，铅笔刀便戳上了他的脸，恰在颧骨上划开一道小小的三角口子——他指给我看了。他痛骂铁路公司，把毒咒一箩筐一箩倒在司机头上，扬言要索赔。就这样他一路抱怨，全然不注意走到了哪里；他的做派在我眼里显得如此可憎，以至于我竟开始欣赏起自己耍他的这场把戏了。

===Original===
Nevertheless my heart beat a little faster as we turned into the street where Lipsius was waiting. A thousand accidents, I thought, might happen. Some chance might bring one of Headley’s friends to meet us; perhaps, though he knew not Chenies Street, he might know the street where I was taking him; in spite of his short-sight he might possibly make out the number, or in a sudden fit of suspicion he might make an inquiry of the policeman at the corner. Thus every step upon the pavement, as we drew nearer to the goal, was to me a pang and a terror, and every approaching passenger carried a certain threat of danger. I gulped down my excitement with an effort, and made shift to say pretty quietly:﻿—

===Chinese===
尽管如此，拐进利普修斯守候的那条街时，我的心跳还是快了几分。我心想，千百种意外都可能发生。也许碰巧会有黑德利的哪个朋友迎面撞见我们；也许他虽不识奇尼街，却认得我正领他走的那条街；也许他眼神再不济，也可能认出门牌；又也许他疑心陡起，会去街角问一名警察。就这样，越接近终点，人行道上的每一步于我都是一阵刺痛、一阵惊惶，每一个迎面走来的行人，都携着几分危险的威胁。我竭力把激动咽下去，勉力装得相当平静地说：——

===Original===
“No. 15, I think you said? That is the third house from this. If you will allow me, I will leave you now; I have been delayed a little, and my way lies on the other side of Tottenham Court Road.”

He snarled out some kind of thanks, and I turned my back and walked swiftly in the opposite direction. A minute or two later, I looked round and saw Mr. Headley standing on the doorstep, and then the door opened and he went in. For my part I gave a sigh of relief, and hastened to get away from the neighborhood and endeavored to enjoy myself in merry company.

===Chinese===
“您说的是 15 号吧？从这儿数是第三幢。您若允许，我就此告辞；我耽搁了一会儿，我的路在托特纳姆宫路那一边。”

他从牙缝里挤出一声道谢，我便转过身，朝相反方向疾步而去。一两分钟后我回头望去，黑德利先生正站在门前台阶上；接着门开了，他走了进去。我这边如释重负地舒了口气，赶忙离开那一带，到快活的伙伴堆里尽力作乐去了。

===Original===
The whole of the next day I kept away from Lipsius. I felt anxious, but I did not know what had happened or what was happening, and a reasonable regard for my own safety told me that I should do well to remain quietly at home. My curiosity, however, to learn the end of the odd drama in which I had played a part stung me to the quick, and late in the evening I made up my mind to go and see how events had turned out. Lipsius nodded when I came in, and asked me if I could give him five minutes’ talk. We went into his room, and he began to walk up and down, and I sat waiting for him to speak.

===Chinese===
第二天一整天我都躲着利普修斯。我心里焦虑，却不知道已经发生了什么、正在发生什么；对自身安全的合理顾虑告诉我，安安静静待在家里才是上策。然而，想知道那出我参与过的怪戏如何收场的好奇心，刺得我坐立难安；深夜时分，我终于拿定主意去看看事情的结果。我进门时利普修斯点了点头，问我能否给他五分钟谈谈。我们进了他的房间，他开始来回踱步，我坐着等他开口。

===Original===
“My dear Mr. Walters,” he said at length, “I congratulate you warmly. Your work was done in the most thorough and artistic manner. You will go far. Look.”

He went to his escritoire and pressed a secret spring, and a drawer flew out, and he laid something on the table. It was a gold coin, and I took it up and examined it eagerly, and read the legend about the figure of the faun.

===Chinese===
“亲爱的沃尔特斯先生，”他终于开口，“我热烈地祝贺您。您这桩差事办得最彻底、最有艺术。您前途无量。看。”

他走到小写字台前，按动一处暗簧，一只抽屉弹了出来；他把一样东西搁在桌上。那是一枚金币，我拿起来急切地细看，读出法翁像旁环绕的铭文。

===Original===
“Victoria,” I said, smiling.

“Yes, it was a great capture, which we owe to you. I had great difficulty in persuading Mr. Headley that a little mistake had been made; that was how I put it. He was very disagreeable, and indeed ungentlemanly about it; didn’t he strike you as a very cross old man?”

===Chinese===
“‘胜利’（Victoria），”我含笑说道。

“不错，这是一场大收获，全亏了您。我好说歹说，才让黑德利先生相信只是出了点小小的差错——我是这么措辞的。他在此事上非常难缠，说句实话，简直没有绅士风度；您不觉得他是个非常暴躁的老头儿么？”

===Original===
I held the coin, admiring the choice and rare design, clear cut as if from the mint; and I thought the fine gold glowed and burned like a lamp.

“And what finally became of Mr. Headley?” I said at last.

===Chinese===
我托着那枚金币，赏玩那精择的、罕见的图案，币纹清晰锋利，宛如新出铸炉；我只觉那足金熠熠生辉，像一盏灯似的燃着。

“那么，黑德利先生最后怎么样了？”我终于问道。

===Original===
Lipsius smiled and shrugged his shoulders.

“What on earth does it matter?” he said. “He might be here, or there, or anywhere; but what possible consequence could it be? Besides, your question rather surprises me. You are an intelligent man, Mr. Walters. Just think it over, and I’m sure you won’t repeat the question.”

===Chinese===
利普修斯微微一笑，耸了耸肩。

“这又有什么要紧？”他说。“他也许在这儿，也许在那儿，也许在随便什么地方；可这又能有什么了不得的干系呢？再说，您这个问题颇叫我诧异。您是个聪明人，沃尔特斯先生。细细想一想，我确信您不会再问第二遍。”

===Original===
“My dear sir,” I said, “I hardly think you are treating me fairly. You have paid me some handsome compliments on my share in the capture, and I naturally wish to know how the matter ended. From what I saw of Mr. Headley, I should think you must have had some difficulty with him.”

He gave me no answer for the moment, but began again to walk up and down the room, apparently absorbed in thought.

===Chinese===
“亲爱的先生，”我说，“我觉得您待我不大公道。您方才还把我在这场收获里的那份夸了又夸，我自然想知道事情如何了结。就我对黑德利先生的观察，我想你们同他打交道，总免不了费些周折吧。”

他一时没有作答，只在屋里重新踱起步来，显然沉浸在思索里。

===Original===
“Well,” he said at last, “I suppose there is something in what you say. We are certainly indebted to you. I have said that I have a high opinion of your intelligence, Mr. Walters. Just look here, will you.”

He opened a door communicating with another room and pointed.

===Chinese===
“好吧，”他终于说，“我想您的话也有几分道理。我们当然欠着您的情。我说过，我对您的智力评价很高，沃尔特斯先生。请到这边来看一看，劳驾。”

他打开一道通向邻室的门，指了指。

===Original===
There was a great box lying on the floor; a queer coffin-shaped thing. I looked at it and saw it was a mummy case like those in the British Museum, vividly painted in the brilliant Egyptian colors, with I knew not what proclamation of dignity or hopes of life immortal. The mummy, swathed about in the robes of death, was lying within, and the face had been uncovered.

===Chinese===
地板上停放着一只大箱；一件棺材形状的怪物。我看了看，认出那是一具木乃伊匣，同大英博物馆里的一样，漆着埃及那种鲜艳夺目的色彩，不知宣示着怎样的尊贵、怎样的长生之望。裹着殓衣的木乃伊躺在里面，脸已经露出来了。

===Original===
“You are going to send this away?” I said, forgetting the question I had put.

“Yes; I have an order from a local museum. Look a little more closely, Mr. Walters.”

===Chinese===
“你们要把这个运走么？”我问，把我先前的问题忘了个干净。

“是的；本地的博物馆向我订了货。再凑近些看，沃尔特斯先生。”

===Original===
Puzzled by his manner, I peered into the face, while he held up the lamp. The flesh was black with the passing of the centuries; but as I looked I saw upon the right cheekbone a small triangular scar, and the secret of the mummy flashed upon me. I was looking at the dead body of the man whom I had decoyed into that house.

===Chinese===
他这副神气叫我摸不着头脑，便凑近端详那张脸，他高擎着灯。皮肉因几个世纪的流逝而发黑；可我正看着，忽见右颧骨上有一道小小的三角疤——木乃伊的秘密电光般击中了我。我望着那具尸身，正是被我诱进那所宅子的人。

===Original===
There was no thought or design of action in my mind. I held the accursed coin in my hand, burning me with a foretaste of hell, and I fled as I would have fled from pestilence and death, and dashed into the street in blind horror, not knowing where I went. I felt the gold coin grasped in my clenched fist, and threw it away, I knew not where, and ran on and on through by-streets and dark ways, till at last I issued out into a crowded thoroughfare, and checked myself. Then, as consciousness returned, I realized my instant peril, and understood what would happen if I fell into the hands of Lipsius. I knew that I had put forth my finger to thwart a relentless mechanism rather than a man; my recent adventure with the unfortunate Mr. Headley had taught me that Lipsius had agents in all quarters, and I foresaw that if I fell into his hands, he would remain true to his doctrine of style, and cause me to die a death of some horrible and ingenious torture. I bent my whole mind to the task of outwitting him and his emissaries, three of whom I knew to have proved their ability for tracking down persons who for various reasons preferred to remain obscure. These servants of Lipsius were two men and a woman, and the woman was incomparably the most subtle and the most deadly. Yet I considered that I too had some portion of craft, and I took my resolve. Since then I have matched myself day by day and hour by hour against the ingenuity of Lipsius and his myrmidons. For a time I was successful; though they beat furiously after me in the covert of London, I remained perdu, and watched with some amusement their frantic efforts to recover the scent lost in two or three minutes. Every lure and wile was put forth to entice me from my hiding-place. I was informed by the medium of the public prints that what I had taken had been recovered, and meetings were proposed in which I might hope to gain a great deal without the slightest risk. I laughed at their endeavors, and began a little to despise the organization I had so dreaded, and ventured more abroad. Not once or twice, but several times, I recognized the two men who were charged with my capture, and I succeeded in eluding them easily at close quarters; and a little hastily I decided that I had nothing to dread, and that my craft was greater than theirs. But in the mean while, while I congratulated myself on my cunning, the third of Lipsius’s emissaries was weaving her nets, and in an evil hour I paid a visit to an old friend, a literary man named Russell, who lived in a quiet street in Bayswater. The woman, as I found out too late, a day or two ago, occupied rooms in the same house, and I was followed and tracked down. Too late, as I have said, I recognized that I had made a fatal mistake, and that I was besieged. Sooner or later I shall find myself in the power of an enemy without pity; and so surely as I leave this house I shall go to receive doom. I hardly dare to guess how it will at last fall upon me. My imagination, always a vivid one, paints to me appalling pictures of the unspeakable torture which I shall probably endure; and I know that I shall die with Lipsius standing near and gloating over the refinements of my suffering and my shame.

===Chinese===
我脑中已没有半点思绪，也谈不上任何打算。我手里攥着那枚该咒的金币，它带着地狱的预感灼烧着我；我逃了，像逃避瘟疫与死亡那样逃了，满怀盲目的恐怖冲到街上，不知去向何处。我觉出金币还攥在紧握的拳头里，便把它扔了，扔到哪儿我也不知道，只顾在僻街暗巷里跑啊跑，直到冲上一条熙攘的大道，才收住脚。随后，神志回转，我登时明白了自己眼下的险境，明白了落入利普修斯之手会遭怎样的处置。我知道，我伸出手指去阻挠的并不是一个人，而是一部没有心肝的机器；同不幸的黑德利先生的最近一场遭遇已经教会我：利普修斯的党羽遍布四方；我也预见到，一旦落进他手里，他必不改他那套“风格”的教义，必叫我在某种精巧而可怖的酷刑中死去。我倾尽全部心智，与他们周旋；他手下的密使，我知道共有三个，个个都已证明自己有本事追踪那些因种种缘故宁愿隐姓埋名的人。利普修斯的这三个仆从，两男一女，而那女的，无疑最狡黠、也最致命。可我自忖自己也有几分狡计，便拿定了主意。自那时起，我便日复一日、时复一时，同利普修斯及其鹰犬的机变斗法。有一阵子我是成功的；他们虽在伦敦的掩蔽深处疯狂扑捕，我却始终隐匿无踪，还带着几分快意，看他们为那两三分钟里丢掉的踪迹气急败坏。他们使出种种钓饵与巧计诱我出洞。借公开报刊之口，他们通知我，说我拿走的东西已经追回；又有会面之约，许我毫无风险而大有渔利。我嗤笑他们的伎俩，渐渐有点小看这个我曾那样畏惧的组织，胆子也大了些，出门多了。不止一两次，而是好几回，我认出了那两个专司缉拿我的男人，并且都能在近逼之际从容脱身；于是我略略轻率地断定：我无可畏惧，我的狡计胜过他们。可就在我自鸣得意于狡计的当口，利普修斯的第三位密使正在织她的网；在一个凶险的时辰，我去访了一位旧友——一个叫拉塞尔的文人，住在贝斯沃特一条安静的街上。那女人，等我发觉——已是一两天前的事了——就住在同一所房子里；我被跟了踪，被盯上了。太迟了，如我所说，我才明白自己犯下了致命的失着，已被团团围困。或早或迟，我总要落入一个毫无怜悯的仇敌掌中；而我一迈出这所房子，便是去领受判决。我不敢猜那劫数最终会怎样临到我头上。我的想象素来活跃，此刻正给我描画一幅幅骇人的图景，描画我很可能领受的那说不出口的酷刑；我知道，我死的时候，利普修斯必立在近旁，把我痛苦与耻辱的种种精微细细玩味，心满意足。

===Original===
Hours, nay, minutes, have become very precious to me. I sometimes pause in the midst of anticipating my tortures, to wonder whether even now I cannot hit upon some supreme stroke, some design of infinite subtlety, to free myself from the toils. But I find that the faculty of combination has left me. I am as the scholar in the old myth, deserted by the power which has helped me hitherto. I do not know when the supreme moment will come, but sooner or later it is inevitable, and before long I shall receive sentence, and from the sentence to execution will not be long.

===Chinese===
时辰，不，分秒，于我都已极其贵重。有时，在预想种种酷刑的间隙，我会停下来寻思：事到如今，我是否还能想出一记奇着，一个精微到极处的盘算，把自己从罗网里解脱出来。可我发现，运思组合的本领已经弃我而去。我就像古时神话里的那位学者，被一直助我的神力抛弃了。我不知道那最后的时刻何时到来，但或早或迟，势所必然；不久我便要领受判决，而从判决到行刑，也为期不远。

---

===Original===
I cannot remain here a prisoner any longer. I shall go out tonight when the streets are full of crowds and clamors, and make a last effort to escape.

===Chinese===
我再不能困在这里做囚徒了。今夜街上人群鼎沸、喧嚣四起之时，我要出去，作最后一次逃亡的努力。

---

===Original===
It was with profound astonishment that Dyson closed the little book, and thought of the strange series of incidents which had brought him into touch with the plots and counterplots connected with the Gold Tiberius. He had bestowed the coin carefully away, and he shuddered at the bare possibility of its place of deposit becoming known to the evil band who seemed to possess such extraordinary sources of information.

It had grown late while he read, and he put the pocketbook away, hoping with all his heart that the unhappy Walters might even at the eleventh hour escape the doom he dreaded.

===Chinese===
戴森合上那本小簿子，怀着深深的骇异，想着那一连串离奇的事件——正是它们把他牵进了围绕提比略金币的种种阴谋与反阴谋。他早已把金币妥善收藏，可一想到那存放之处万一泄露给这伙似乎拥有非凡消息来源的恶党，他便不寒而栗。

他一路读来，时间已晚；他把记事簿收好，衷心盼着那不幸的沃尔特斯纵然到了最后关头，也能逃脱他所惧怕的那场劫数。

## Adventure of the Deserted Residence / 废宅奇遇

===Original===
“A wonderful story, as you say; an extraordinary sequence and play of coincidence. I confess that your expressions when you first showed me the Gold Tiberius were not exaggerated. But do you think that Walters has really some fearful fate to dread?”

“I cannot say. Who can presume to predict events when life itself puts on the robe of coincidence and plays at drama? Perhaps we have not yet reached the last chapter in the queer story. But, look, we are drawing near to the verge of London; there are gaps, you see, in the serried ranks of brick, and a vision of green fields beyond.”

===Chinese===
“确是个奇妙的故事，正如你所说；巧合的连缀与搬演，非同寻常。我承认，你当初把提比略金币拿给我看时，你的说法并无夸张。可你觉得，沃尔特斯当真有什么可怕的劫数要担惊么？”

“我说不上。当生活自己披上巧合的法衣、演起戏文来时，谁还敢妄言预断世事？这出怪戏，也许我们还没读到最后一章。不过你看，我们已经快到伦敦的边沿了；你瞧，砖石的密阵里已豁出缺口，缺口外隐约是一片绿野。”

===Original===
Dyson had persuaded the ingenious Mr. Phillipps to accompany him on one of those aimless walks to which he was himself so addicted. Starting from the very heart of London, they had made their way westward through the stony avenues, and were now just emerging from the red lines of an extreme suburb, and presently the half-finished road ended, a quiet lane began, and they were beneath the shade of elm-trees. The yellow autumn sunlight that had lit up the bare distance of the suburban street now filtered down through the boughs of the trees and shone on the glowing carpet of fallen leaves, and the pools of rain glittered and shot back the gleam of light. Over all the broad pastures there was peace and the happy rest of autumn before the great winds begin, and afar off, London lay all vague and immense amidst the veiling mist; here and there a distant window catching the sun and kindling with fire, and a spire gleaming high, and below the streets in shadow, and the turmoil of life. Dyson and Phillipps walked on in silence beneath the high hedges, till at a turn of the lane they saw a mouldering and ancient gate standing open, and the prospect of a house at the end of a moss-grown carriage drive.

===Chinese===
戴森说动了那位好机巧的菲利普斯先生，让他陪自己作又一次漫无目的的闲逛——这类闲逛，他自己正是乐此不疲。两人从伦敦的腹地出发，一路向西穿过石头的大街，此刻刚刚走出远郊那红色的街阵；不多时，那条未完工的大路到了尽头，一条安静的小巷开始了，他们已走在榆树的荫下。方才照亮郊区街道空旷远景的黄色秋阳，此刻从枝柯间筛落下来，照在落叶织成的发亮的地毯上，雨后的水洼闪闪反光，迸回着天光。辽阔的草场上，一派安详，是大风将至之前秋天那种幸福的憩息；远处，伦敦在帷幕般的雾霭里横陈着，一片朦胧，一片浩大；这里那里，一扇远处的窗子接住太阳，燃起一点火光，一座尖塔在高处闪烁；再往下，是阴影中的街道，与生活的扰攘。戴森与菲利普斯在高高的树篱下默默走着，在小巷一个转弯处，望见一扇朽坏的古门敞着，门内一条生了苔的车道尽头，隐然有一所房子。

===Original===
“There is a survival for you,” said Dyson; “it has come to its last days, I imagine. Look how the laurels have grown gaunt, and weedy, and black, and bare, beneath; look at the house, covered with yellow wash and patched with green damp. Why, the very notice-board which informs all and singular that the place is to be let has cracked and half fallen.”

“Suppose we go in and see it,” said Phillipps. “I don’t think there is anybody about.”

===Chinese===
“这就叫遗存，”戴森说，“依我看，它已到风烛残年。瞧那些月桂，长成了精瘦的模样，芜杂，发黑，底下光秃秃；再瞧这房子，罩着黄色粉刷，斑斑驳驳补着绿霉。嘿，连那块昭告各色人等、此屋待租的招帖牌，也裂了、塌了半边。”

“咱们进去看看如何，”菲利普斯说，“我看附近一个人也没有。”

===Original===
They turned up the drive, and walked slowly towards this remnant of old days. It was a large straggling house, with curved wings at either end, and behind a series of irregular roofs and projections, showing that the place had been added to at diverse dates; the two wings were roofed in cupola fashion, and at one side, as they came nearer, they could see a stable-yard, and a clock turret with a bell, and the dark masses of gloomy cedars. Amidst all the lineaments of dissolution, there was but one note of contrast: the sun was setting beyond the elm-trees, and all the west and the south were in flames, and on the upper windows of the house the glow shone reflected, and it seemed as if blood and fire were mingled. Before the yellow front of the mansion, stained, as Dyson had remarked, with gangrenous patches, green and blackening, stretched what once had been, no doubt, a well-kept lawn, but it was now rough and ragged, and nettles and great docks, and all manner of coarse weeds, struggled in the places of the flowerbeds. The urns had fallen from their pillars beside the walk, and lay broken in shards upon the ground, and everywhere from grass-plot and path a fungoid growth had sprung up and multiplied, and lay dank and slimy like a festering sore upon the earth. In the middle of the rank grass of the lawn was a desolate fountain; the rim of the basin was crumbling and pulverized with decay, and within, the water stood stagnant, with green scum for the lilies that had once bloomed there; and rust had eaten into the bronze flesh of the Triton that stood in the middle, and the conch-shell he held was broken.

===Chinese===
两人转上车道，朝这件旧日的残存缓缓走去。这是所大而散漫的房子，两端各带一座弧形厢翼；后面是一列参差的屋顶与凸出部，可见历年历代都有增建；两翼的顶做成穹顶亭子式；走近一侧，还望得见一处马厩院、一座带钟铃的角塔，和雪松一团一团黑沉沉的暗影。在这满目崩解的形貌之间，只有一处异色：太阳正落到榆树后面，整个西天与南天烈焰熊熊，宅子上层的窗子映着余晖，仿佛血与火交融在了一处。宅子黄色的正面——戴森已点过——染着坏疽般的黑绿斑块；正面前想必曾是一片收拾得很好的草坪，如今却粗粝荒芜，荨麻、大酸模和各式粗贱的野草，在花床的旧址上恣意挣扎。小径两旁柱子上的瓮早跌了下来，碎成瓦砾散在地上；草地与甬道上，到处滋生蔓延着一种菌类，湿冷黏滑地摊着，像大地上溃烂的疮。草坪乱草的中央，是一座荒凉的喷泉：池沿已酥烂成粉；池水停滞，绿苔替代了当年开过的睡莲；锈已蚀进池心那尊特里同（Triton）铜铸的皮肉，他手里捧着的螺壳也破了。

===Original===
“Here,” said Dyson, “one might moralize over decay and death. Here all the stage is decked out with the symbols of dissolution; the cedarn gloom and twilight hangs heavy around us, and everywhere within the pale dankness has found a harbor, and the very air is changed and brought to accord with the scene. To me, I confess, this deserted house is as moral as a graveyard, and I find something sublime in that lonely Triton, deserted in the midst of his water-pool. He is the last of the gods; they have left him and he remembers the sound of water falling on water, and the days that were sweet.”

===Chinese===
“在这儿，”戴森说，“人尽可以对朽坏与死亡发一番道德的议论。这儿整座舞台，都用崩解的符号装点齐了；雪松的幽暗与暝色沉沉压在我们四周；圈墙之内，湿气处处找到了停泊的港口，连空气也变了质，应和着眼前这景象。我承认，在我眼里，这座荒宅同墓地一样富于道德的意涵；而那孤零零的特里同，被遗弃在水池中央，我更觉得他身上有种崇高的东西。他是最后的神；众神都已离弃他，而他还记得水落在水上的声音，记得那些甜美的日子。”

===Original===
“I like your reflections extremely,” said Phillipps, “but I may mention that the door of the house is open.”

“Let us go in then.”

===Chinese===
“你这一番玄想我极喜欢，”菲利普斯说，“不过容我提醒一句：房子的门开着。”

“那我们就进去吧。”

===Original===
The door was just ajar, and they passed into the mouldy hall, and looked in at a room on one side. It was a large room, going far back, and the rich old red flock paper was peeling from the walls in long strips, and blackened with vague patches of rising damp; the ancient clay, the dank reeking earth rising up again, and subduing all the work of men’s hands after the conquest of many years. And the floor was thick with the dust of decay, and the painted ceiling fading from all gay colors and light fancies of cupids in a career, and disfigured with sores of dampness, seemed transmuted into other work. No longer the amorini chased one another pleasantly, with limbs that sought not to advance, and hands that merely simulated the act of grasping at the wreathed flowers, but it appeared some savage burlesque of the old careless world and of its cherished conventions, and the dance of the loves had become a dance of Death; black pustules and festering sores swelled and clustered on fair limbs, and smiling faces showed corruption, and the fairy blood had boiled with the germs of foul disease; it was a parable of the leaven working, and worms devouring for a banquet the heart of the rose.

===Chinese===
门只虚掩着一道缝；他们走进发霉的门厅，向一侧的房间里张望。那是间大屋子，进深很远；贵重的旧红植绒墙纸成条成条地从墙上剥落，泛起一片片黑渍——古老的泥土，那阴冷濡湿的大地，历经多年征服之后又重新升上来，压倒了人手的一切造作。地板上厚厚积着朽坏的红尘；彩绘的天花板，那上头种种明快的颜色、成队小爱神轻情驰逐的意匠，正从一切愉悦里褪去，又给湿气的疮疤弄得面目全非，仿佛点化成了另一种作品。不再是爱神们舒徐地互相追逐——那双腿并不真想前进、那双手只作势去攀折花环的样子——倒像是旧日那个无忧无虑的世界及其珍视的成规，被人做了一番野蛮的戏仿；爱神的轮舞成了死亡的轮舞；黑色的痘疹与溃疮在美好的肢体上肿起、簇生，含笑的脸上露出腐坏，仙子的血液里沸腾着秽病的病菌；这是一则寓言：酵在暗中发作，蛆虫把玫瑰的心当作筵席，一口一口吞噬。

===Original===
Strangely, under the painted ceiling, against the decaying walls, two old chairs still stood alone, the sole furniture of the empty place. High-backed, with curving arms and twisted legs, covered with faded gold leaf, and upholstered in tattered damask, they too were a part of the symbolism, and struck Dyson with surprise. “What have we here?” he said. “Who has sat in these chairs? Who, clad in peach-bloom satin, with lace ruffles and diamond buckles, all golden, *a conté fleurettes* to his companion? Phillipps, we are in another age. I wish I had some snuff to offer you, but failing that, I beg to offer you a seat, and we will sit and smoke tobacco. A horrid practice, but I am no pedant.”

===Chinese===
说来奇怪，就在这彩绘的天花板下，靠着朽坏的墙壁，竟还独自立着两把旧椅子，是这空屋里仅存的家什。高高的椅背，弯弯的扶手，绞缠的椅腿，覆着褪色的金箔，绷着破碎的花缎椅面——它们也是这象征体系的一部分，叫戴森吃了一惊。“这是什么呀？”他说。“谁在这椅子上坐过？谁又曾身穿桃红缎衣、缀着蕾丝的褶边、蹬着钻石的鞋扣，通身金光，对身旁的伴当絮絮地说情话（*a conté fleurettes*）？菲利普斯，我们这是进了另一个世纪。可惜我无鼻烟可以敬你；既然如此，请容我敬你一个座位，我们坐下抽袋烟。烟是个可憎的习惯，可我并非道学先生。”

===Original===
They sat down on the queer old chairs, and looked out of the dim and grimy panes to the ruined lawn, and the fallen urns, and the deserted Triton.

Presently Dyson ceased his imitation of eighteenth century airs; he no longer pulled forward imaginary ruffles, or tapped a ghostly snuffbox.

===Chinese===
两人在古怪的旧椅上坐下，透过昏暗污秽的窗玻璃，望着荒废的草坪、跌碎的瓮，和那被离弃的特里同。

不多时，戴森收起了他对十八世纪做派的模仿；他不再去抻那想象中的褶边，也不再叩击那只虚空的鼻烟盒。

===Original===
“It’s a foolish fancy,” he said at last, “but I keep thinking I hear a noise like someone groaning. Listen; no, I can’t hear it now. There it is again! Did you notice it, Phillipps?”

“No, I can’t say I heard anything. But I believe that old places like this are like shells from the shore, ever echoing with noises. The old beams, mouldering piecemeal, yield a little and groan, and such a house as this I can fancy all resonant at night with voices, the voices of matter so slowly and so surely transformed into other shapes; the voice of the worm that gnaws at last the very heart of the oak; the voice of stone grinding on stone, and the voice of the conquest of time.”

===Chinese===
“一个痴念罢了，”他终于说，“可我总疑心听见一种声响，像有人在呻吟。听；——不，现在又听不见了。又来了！你留意到了么，菲利普斯？”

“没有，我不敢说听见了什么。不过我相信，这样的老宅就像海边拾来的螺壳，永远回响着种种声响。老梁木一处一处地朽，一点一点地让劲，呻吟一声；这样一所房子，我可以想象它入夜后满宅都是声音——物质如此迟缓又如此确然地化为别种形相时发出的声音；蛆虫终于蛀到橡木心脏的声音；石头与石头相磨的声音，还有时间完成征服的声音。”

===Original===
They sat still in the old armchairs and grew graver in the musty ancient air﻿—the air of a hundred years ago.

“I don’t like the place,” said Phillipps, after a long pause. “To me it seems as if there were a sickly, unwholesome smell about it, a smell of something burning.”

===Chinese===
两人静坐在旧扶手椅里，在霉浊古老的空气中——一百年前的空气中——神色渐渐凝重起来。

好半天，菲利普斯才说：“我不喜欢这地方。在我鼻子里，这里仿佛浮动着一股病态的、不洁的气味，一股什么东西烧焦的气味。”

===Original===
“You are right; there is an evil odor here. I wonder what it is! Hark! Did you hear that?”

A hollow sound, a noise of infinite sadness and infinite pain broke in upon the silence; and the two men looked fearfully at one another, horror and the sense of unknown things glimmering in their eyes.

===Chinese===
“你说得对；这儿是有一股邪气。会是什么呢！听！你听见没有？”

一声空洞的声响，一种怀着无尽悲哀、无尽痛苦的声响，打破了寂静；两人骇然对望，眼里闪着恐怖，和对未知之物的预感。

===Original===
“Come,” said Dyson, “we must see into this,” and they went into the hall and listened in the silence.

“Do you know,” said Phillipps, “it seems absurd, but I could almost fancy that the smell is that of burning flesh.”

===Chinese===
“来，”戴森说，“这事非查个明白不可。”两人走进门厅，在寂静里侧耳倾听。

“你可知，”菲利普斯说，“这话听着荒唐，可我几乎觉得，这气味像烧焦的人肉。”

===Original===
They went up the hollow-sounding stairs, and the odor became thick and noisome, stifling the breath; and a vapor, sickening as the smell of the chamber of death, choked them. A door was open and they entered the large upper room, and clung hard to one another, shuddering at the sight they saw.

===Chinese===
两人踏上空洞作响的楼梯，气味愈来愈浓浊恶臭，遏得人喘不上气；一股蒸气，令人作呕如死室的气味，呛得他们透不过气来。一扇门开着，他们走进楼上那间大屋，看见眼前的景象，骇得紧紧靠在一起，浑身战栗。

===Original===
A naked man was lying on the floor, his arms and legs stretched wide apart, and bound to pegs that had been hammered into the boards. The body was torn and mutilated in the most hideous fashion, scarred with the marks of red-hot irons, a shameful ruin of the human shape. But upon the middle of the body a fire of coals was smouldering; the flesh had been burned through. The man was dead, but the smoke of his torment mounted still, a black vapor.

===Chinese===
一个赤裸的男人躺在地板上，四肢大张，绑在钉进地板的木桩上。躯体已被撕毁、残毁得骇人至极，烙着烧红的烙铁留下的疤，人的形体成了一片可耻的废墟。而在躯体正中，一小堆炭火还在阴燃；那里的皮肉已被烧穿。人已经死了，可他受刑的烟仍在升腾——一缕黑汽。

===Original===
“The young man with spectacles,” said Mr. Dyson.

===Chinese===
“戴眼镜的年轻人，”戴森先生说。
